\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\iint\limits_{\Sigma_{+}}\left(\left\langle\frac{\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right\rangle_{\partial\Omega}-\frac{\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega}}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{\nu}_{\varepsilon}\,.

The renormalized boundary condition
(
10.23
)
yields that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\gamma_{\!+}\tilde{g}_{\epsilon}-L\gamma_{\!-}\tilde{g}_{\epsilon})-\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}}

(11.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}(\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}+\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2})(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon}^{2})}+\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\,.

Thus, after simple calculations, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\gamma\tilde{g}_{\epsilon}\gamma g^{\tau,k}_{\varepsilon,4}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x}

(11.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\iint\limits_{\Sigma_{+}}\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon}(\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}+\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2})(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon}^{2})}L\gamma_{-}g^{\pm}_{k,\epsilon,2}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})M\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x}

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\iint\limits_{\Sigma_{+}}\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega}(\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}+\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2})(1+\varepsilon^{2}\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega}^{2})}L\gamma_{-}g^{\pm}_{k,\epsilon,2}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})M\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x}

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}\iint\limits_{\Sigma_{+}}\left\langle\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}(\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}+\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2})(1+\varepsilon^{2}\langle\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\partial\Omega}^{2})}\right\rangle_{\partial\Omega}L\gamma_{-}g^{\pm}_{k,\epsilon,2}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})M\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x}\,.

The
a priori
estimates from boundary yields that all the three
terms on the right-hand side of (
11.26
) are bounded in in

L^{p}_{loc}(\mathrm{d}t)
for
p>1
. In deed, the integral of the
first term over
[t_{1}\,,t_{2}]
is bounded by

\displaystyle\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\iint\limits_{\Sigma_{+}}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon}}\frac{\gamma^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\epsilon}^{2})^{1/4}}\frac{\sqrt{\alpha_{\epsilon}}(\varepsilon\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}^{2}_{\varepsilon}}\frac{(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\epsilon}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\epsilon}^{2})^{3/4}}L\gamma_{-}g^{\pm}_{k,\epsilon,2}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})M\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x}

(11.27)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\iint\limits_{\Sigma_{+}}\frac{\alpha_{\epsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\gamma^{(2)}_{\varepsilon}\frac{\varepsilon\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\varepsilon}^{2})(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon}^{2})}L\gamma_{-}g^{\pm}_{k,\epsilon,2}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})M\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x}

Note that

\frac{\sqrt{\alpha_{\epsilon}}(\varepsilon\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}^{2}_{\varepsilon}}\quad\mbox{and}\!\quad\frac{(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\epsilon}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\gamma_{\!+}\hat{g}_{\varepsilon})}{(1+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma_{\!+}g_{\epsilon}^{2})^{3/4}}\mathbf{1}_{\gamma_{\!+}G_{\varepsilon}\leq 2\langle G_{\varepsilon}\rangle_{{\partial\Omega}}\leq 4\gamma_{\!+}G_{\varepsilon}}

are bounded. Furthermore,
L\gamma_{-}g^{\pm}_{k,\epsilon,2}(v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})
is bounded in

L^{q}((v\!\cdot\!\mathrm{n})M\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{x})
for

q\geq 2
. Now, the estimates (
10.16
), (
10.18
) of
Lemma
10.2
imply that the first term in (
11.27
)
is bounded in
L^{p}_{loc}(\mathrm{d}t)
, the second term vanishes as

\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Similarly, using Lemma
10.1
, Lemma

10.2
, we can prove that the integrals over
[t_{1}\,,t_{2}]
of
the second and third terms of (
11.26
) can be decomposed
into two terms, one is bounded in
L^{p}_{loc}(\mathrm{d}t)
, the
other vanishes in
L^{1}_{loc}(\mathrm{d}t)
. Thus we proved the Lemma

11.1
.

∎
